  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
BRANCH 1: [Use the textual qualifier “at the mouth of” to infer a boundary contact]  
The phrase “at the mouth of fox river” indicates that Green Bay is situated at the point where the Fox River empties into Green Bay (the lake). The mouth is a single point on the river’s endpoint. Therefore the city’s boundary meets the river only at that point; the river does not pass through the city’s interior.  
Suggested predicate: touches  

BRANCH 2: [Test the possibility of a containment relation]  
Could Green Bay be “within” the Fox River? The river is a LineString, not a polygon, so a city cannot be fully inside it. Conversely, could the river “contain” the city? The river is a line and cannot enclose a polygon. Thus neither contains nor within applies. The only plausible relation is a boundary contact.
